# Planning Guidance

## Purpose

Before implementation tasks are defined, the Architect should establish a feature
map for the first AssetOps product sequence. The feature map should explain how
visible UI capabilities depend on product, domain, data, and architecture
capabilities.

Do not start by listing implementation tasks. Start by understanding the screens
and the causal prerequisites that make them truthful.

## Source Material

For this replan, direction comes only from `Docs/simulator_design_v4.md`
(mechanisms) and `Docs/mini-grid-demo-architecture-and-roadmap.md` (demo path).
This replaces the previous instruction to start sequencing from UI references.

`.ai/FEATURE_MAP.md` maps the corrected sources onto the built-state record.
Read `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` and completed tasks for what exists, not earlier
planning reviews for direction. Existing canonical UI layouts remain useful
presentation constraints; they cannot invent capability or reorder the demo.

## Architect Hats

The Architect may wear these hats as needed:

- Product: define what user-visible capability each feature provides.
- Architecture: identify durable boundaries, contracts, and dependency
  direction.
- UI/UX: identify screen flows, information hierarchy, and user-verifiable
  states.
- Domain: identify site, component, device, simulator, gateway, evidence, and
  operational semantics.

These are hats for analysis, not separate standing roles.

## Core Planning Principle

Plan features in causal order.

If a screen displays a fact, state, or diagram, identify what must exist before
that display can be meaningful. Then decide whether that prerequisite should be
its own UI-verifiable slice or part of a larger slice.

For example, the Simulator Lab screen shows site information, a single-line
diagram, devices and sensors, gateway output, events, run state, and simulation
controls. Those features imply earlier or parallel capabilities:

- Sites must be managed before a simulator run can target a site.
- Site components must be configured before a single-line diagram can be shown.
- Devices and sensors must be defined before device tables or gateway output can
  be meaningful.
- Scenario and event definitions must exist before scheduled events or injected
  events can be shown.
- Simulator run state must remain separate from product evidence until an
  observation is published through a boundary.
- Site Details can show operational evidence only after the evidence contract
  exists.

## Feature Versus Task

A feature is a complete product function or capability that a user can recognize
across one or more screens. A feature may require several tasks before it is
complete.

A task is a reviewable implementation slice that delivers or materially advances
one UI-verifiable part of a feature. A task should be small enough to implement
and review independently, but it does not need to complete the whole feature.

Feature examples:

- Site management and configuration.
- Simulator Lab run setup and execution.
- Device and sensor management.
- Gateway publishing and ingestion visibility.
- Evidence-backed operational findings.

Task examples:

- Add basic site identity and parameters to the Foundation screen.
- Show configured site components in the single-line diagram area.
- Let Simulator Lab select a configured site for a run.
- Publish one simulator observation into Site Details.
- Show one finding with supporting evidence.

Do not use tasks as the first planning object. Start with features and their
causal prerequisites, then break each feature into tasks only when the feature
sequence is understood.

## Feature Map Output

The Architect's first output should be a concise feature map, not a task list.
It should include:

- Feature areas visible in the canonical screens.
- Causal prerequisites for each feature area.
- Candidate tasks under each feature, only after the feature boundary and
  dependencies are clear.
- Product/domain semantics that must be decided before implementation.
- Architecture seams and contracts that must be protected.
- UI-verifiable outcomes that could become vertical slices.
- Open questions that should be resolved before task breakdown.

## From Feature Map To Tasks

After the feature map is reviewed, the Planner breaks features into tasks.
Each task should:

- Produce or heavily contribute to a UI-verifiable outcome.
- Identify the feature it contributes to.
- Name the screen behavior it enables.
- Include only the minimum backend, domain, data, UI, and tests needed for that
  outcome.
- Preserve simulator/product/evidence boundaries.
- Mark user-review checkpoints where product direction, UI/UX, domain
  semantics, or evidence interpretation changes.

Task specs should be concise implementation guardrails. `.ai/WORKFLOW.md`,
Task Spec Size, owns the sizing rule and is the only place it is stated: size
ceilings by slice type, no minimum, and the cold-start test a spec must pass.
If more detail is needed, split the task or move durable reasoning into the
feature map, decisions, or architecture documents.

Task specs should not prescribe component factoring, helper names, styling
mechanics, test structure, or local code organization below established
boundaries unless the choice protects a named seam.

During planning review, scan new task specs for defensive repetition. Repeated
negative scope lists, exact-mechanics requirements, and copy-preservation rules
should either be tied to a named seam, moved into shared guidance, or removed.

Do not treat later capabilities as out of scope for the product. Treat them as
sequenced features whose prerequisites must be made real in the right order.

## Scoped Handoffs

This file stays general. When the Architect has feature-level guidance for a
specific run of slices, it lives in its own scoped file, is named here while it
is live, and is deleted when its last slice closes out.

## Findings

### F1 - Medium - S6-03 does not account for S6-02a's new Action event kinds (`tasks/S6-03-site-work-list.md:3,11,13`)

S6-03 says "S6-02 is queue order only", branches from merged main, selects the latest event by `(recorded_at,id)` and forbids an "invented verification result". S6-02a (queued ahead) alters `action_event_kind_check` to add `RESOLVED` and `RESOLUTION_WITHDRAWN` (`task/S6-02a-verification-core:backend/migrations/versions/0019_verification_core.py:212-230`) and extends `actions/lifecycle.py` reduction (`.../actions/service.py:24-25,53-77` on that branch). If S6-02a merges first, the index's "latest selected event" will often be a SYSTEM `RESOLVED`/`RESOLUTION_WITHDRAWN` event; the packet neither says the reduced lifecycle must come from the shared `actions.lifecycle.reduce` (so it absorbs the S6-02a extension) nor whether a persisted RESOLVED event is shown as recorded state (allowed - it is reviewed S6-02a output, not a v6.9 inference) versus hidden (which would misstate the Action). Either merge order can produce a list whose state disagrees with Action detail. Fix: state that lifecycle is reduced by the same `actions.lifecycle` reducer as `service.action`, that list state must equal Action detail at the same fence (already a parity test, `:19`), and that if S6-02a has merged the fixture includes a RESOLVED and a RESOLUTION_WITHDRAWN row shown as recorded events, never as a verdict derived by the list.

### F2 - Medium - S6-03 walkthrough omits the Performance comparison that releases Finding reads (`tasks/S6-03-site-work-list.md:24-26`)

The walkthrough must open "exact detail/event links after reload", and rows carry exact origin Finding/FindingRevision links. S6-01b's walkthrough had to run the Performance comparison in the private tree "so the Finding reads are released" (`tools/s601b_action_walkthrough.py:19,193-198`); without it the FindingRevision destinations read "not released". `prepare` here only "persists positive and assessed-zero windows via normal Action creation/event path" and does not name the builder. Fix: say `prepare` reuses `backend/tests/s6_action_records.py::build` (the `D-action-records` producer) the way `tools/s601b_action_walkthrough.py` does, then runs the Performance comparison, and `attest` fails if the comparison did not pass. (Reuse of the s601b private-tree/marker pattern keeps this cheap.)

### F3 - Medium - S7-01b walkthrough cannot show the case set it promises (`tasks/S7-01b-health-ui.md:20-22`)

`prepare` "publishes the six S7-01a cases ... plus never-received, no-source and configured-but-missing battery" and the owner opens one URL, `/sites/KOBO-001/health`. Three reachability gaps:
1. The read shows only the latest diagnostic per gateway (`postgres_gateway_access.py:184-187`), so six sequential cases on one gateway show one state. The packet must say the cases are spread over distinct registered gateways (or provide a `step <case>` command that advances one gateway through them).
2. `CURRENT` is `received_at` within `2 x cadence` of the real server clock (`gateway_access.py:50,293-297`; default cadence 60 s, `0006_gateway_access.py:39`), so any "current heartbeat" and "old facts freshly received" case published by `prepare` is `STALE` about two minutes later. The walkthrough needs a `heartbeat`/`deliver` command run immediately before the owner looks (or `serve` delivers it), using the authenticated central path.
3. `NO_REGISTERED_SOURCE` and unbound/absent battery cannot exist on KOBO-001 alongside its registered gateway and configured battery; they need a second prepared Site, and the walkthrough must print its URLs.
Fix: name per-case gateways or a step command, a fresh-receipt command, and the second Site with its printed health and asset URLs.

### F4 - Medium - S3-02a does not compose the existing Performance cutover gate (`tasks/S3-02a-capability-read.md:9,16`)

Every existing S3 read goes through `open_scope`, which refuses `409 PERFORMANCE_CUTOVER_CLOSED` until the Performance comparison passes (`backend/assetops_backend/performance_api.py:51-61`); the S4 index takes the same `Gate` (`finding_index_api.py:28`). The S3-02a refusal list is otherwise exhaustive (Site/revision/window/rule/lineage, 401/403/404) but omits the closed gate, so a new module-level route following the packet literally could serve a positive claim ceiling while the cutover is closed - a fail-open around the existing gate. Fix: require the new route to apply the same Performance gate (typed 409, never `UNASSESSED` or empty) and add a closed-gate case to `test_s3_claim_limit_postgres.py`.

### F5 - Medium - S3-02b walkthrough omits gate setup and names no reusable fixture (`tasks/S3-02b-claim-limit-ui.md:18-20`)

1. The Evidence Analysis page and every exact Evidence drill-down go through the S2 Evidence cutover (`409 AWAITING_EVIDENCE_MIGRATION` until `assetops_backend.evidence_migration run` passes, `backend/assetops_backend/evidence_api.py:205-206,237-245`), and the S3 reads need the Performance comparison (F4). This is the same setup step the S5 Overview walkthrough needed (`tools/s5_overview_walkthrough.py:174-177,189`). `prepare` names neither.
2. `prepare` "reuses the reviewed S3-02a public-ingestion fixture", but S3-02a defines only test files, no builder or manifest a tool can call. Either S3-02a names a reusable builder (like `backend/tests/s6_action_records.py::build`) or S3-02b copies the reviewed `assetops_s3cases` producer DB as `tools/s3_dispatch_walkthrough.py:6-10` does.
3. The URL shape `/sites/KOBO-001/evidence/analysis?date=&assessment_revision_id=` does not exist; the current route is `/sites/{id}/evidence?view=analysis&assessment=` (`frontend/src/shell/operatorSiteRoutes.ts:108-122`). The packet hedges ("wrapper must print authoritative route"), so this part alone would be Low.
Fix: add the S2 Evidence comparison and Performance comparison steps to `prepare` (and `attest` fails if either did not pass), name the fixture source, and cite the real route.

### F6 - Low - Identity seam not cited for the two reused route families (`tasks/S7-01a-health-read.md:7,11`; `tasks/S3-02a-capability-read.md:7,9`)

Both packets require 401/403/404, but the routes they tell the implementer to reuse have no request-level access check: `GET /api/sites/{site_id}/gateway-health` takes no `Request` (`gateway_api.py:93-95`) and performance `open_scope(site_code)` has no access callback (`performance_api.py:51`). The existing seam is `finding_api.Access`/`development_access` as used by `finding_index_api.py:28,44-50`. Citing it avoids an implementer extending the unauthenticated S2 route in place (the S7-01a Architect question already points to a new versioned DTO).

### F7 - Low - `STALE_PROJECTION` may have no producer in S7-01a (`tasks/S7-01a-health-read.md:9,17`; `tasks/S7-01b-health-ui.md:15`)

The ruling makes `STALE_PROJECTION` apply "if any derived read lags" (`architect-W1-remaining-answers.md:17`). Site Health reads the central store directly; if no projection is introduced, the required "stale-projection cases" are untestable and invite an invented projection. Say: if the read has no derived projection, record `STALE_PROJECTION` as not applicable and test only the inconsistent-selection refusal.

### F8 - Low - Layout evidence not mentioned for dense lists (`tasks/S6-03-site-work-list.md:20`, `tasks/S7-01b-health-ui.md:15`, `tasks/S3-02b-claim-limit-ui.md:13`)

All three UI packets are dense table/list surfaces at 1440/390, but none asks for `tools/layout-evidence.mjs` or a `not run` record with its precondition, as S6-02b does (`tasks/S6-02b-verification-ui.md:24`). Process only; not a visual finding.

### F9 - Low - S6-03 has no default window for an unparameterised Work entry (`tasks/S6-03-site-work-list.md:13`)

The URL carries `from`/`to`, but entering Site Work from navigation without them is undefined (default window, or a typed "choose a window" state). Not a fail-open (an unbounded window is a refusal), but the implementer will need to choose; one line avoids a review round.

## Architect questions - are the recommendations safe?

1. **S6-03** (cursor fence over all scoped Actions/events, including off-page rows): **safe**, and fails closed. Clarify "scoped" means Site plus selected window (the ruling's "max included" fence), not the whole Site, to avoid spurious invalidation. Residual risk to carry: a max-`recorded_at` fence alone can miss a later-committing event stamped before the fence if `recorded_at` is a transaction-start time; including the included-row and event counts in the fence closes that cheaply.
2. **S7-01a** (versioned fact-age/trust DTO, no in-place mutation of S2 `gateway-health`; registration and diagnostics in one snapshot): **safe**. Note the existing `gateway_health` issues per-gateway statements (`postgres_gateway_access.py:174-187`); the new read must run them in one repeatable-read transaction for the snapshot claim to hold.
3. **S7-01b** (asset route keyed by configured effective asset ID; typed `NOT_CONFIGURED` Site entry when no battery): **safe**. Add that an asset ID outside the effective basis (foreign, or only in a superseded Foundation revision) is a 404/typed refusal, never a healthy or Unknown row for the old asset.
4. **S3-02a** (role coverage as exact interval spans plus duration, one versioned rule ID; never aggregate away a missing/conflicted subspan): **safe**; it is the fail-closed reading of the ruling.
5. **S3-02b** (keep S2 explanation as its own section; render S3 ceiling only when a selected assessment revision is explicit): **safe** (absence is not a claim). The UI should say why no ceiling is shown (`UNASSESSED` or "no assessment selected") rather than omit the section silently, per the ruling's `UNASSESSED` state.

S4-04's `backend/assetops_backend/finding_index_api.py:3,40` is the scoped index precedent, not an Action producer.

## Read and UI contract

- One consistent DB snapshot selects `created_at >= from AND created_at < to` and latest event by `(recorded_at,id)`, never `occurred_at`. Return row key `(tenant_id,site_id,action_id)`, code, creation time, origin Finding and FindingRevision IDs, selected event ID/recorded time, reduced lifecycle, canonical and exact-event Action links. Order `created_at DESC, action_id ASC`; bounded page and opaque cursor bind tenant, Site, window, selection fence and last sort key.
- Echo scope/display timezone, `read_at`, max included creation/event-recorded fence or explicit zero-row fence, total and next cursor. `CURRENT` only after successful scoped read. Zero means no persisted Actions created in this Site/window. A changed fence invalidates page 2 and requires reload from page 1. Preserve historical Action detail separately.
- 401 unauthenticated, 403 known forbidden, 404 foreign/nonexistent Site or root. Invalid/unbounded window, changed cursor, unavailable store or inconsistent snapshot is typed refusal, never empty success. UI keeps Site/window in URL, shows count/page basis, exact FindingRevision and ActionEvent links, Unknown/error distinctly, native keyboard navigation and no invented verification result.

No UI/panel promotion in this part.

## Verified seams and new contract

Existing central diagnostic selection: `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_gateway_access.py:172-194`; cadence contact and diagnostic fields: `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/gateway_access.py:289-343`; `GET /api/sites/{site_id}/gateway-health`: `backend/assetops_backend/gateway_api.py:38,93-109`; existing client: `frontend/src/sites/gatewayHealthClient.ts:3,68`. Existing S1 asset/basis/binding tables: `backend/migrations/versions/0001_foundation_identity.py:95,149`, `backend/migrations/versions/0002_signal_bindings.py:37,65`. **New:** two-clock Site source-health interpretation/read and Site/asset-scoped configured-battery boundary read/API; neither is a physical health assessment.

Site read resolves registered gateway/source IDs, most recent centrally stored diagnostic per gateway, local sequence, diagnostic ID, server `received_at`, reported `observed_at`, cadence, read time, source watermark, source-specific contact time where supplied, queue depth/oldest age, clock offset/trust and disk pressure. Receipt freshness `CURRENT|STALE|UNKNOWN` is distinct from reported-fact age/trust and `STALE_PROJECTION`. Fresh receipt of old facts may have current gateway contact and old/Unknown facts. Gateway contact never proves source-device contact. No registered gateway = `NO_REGISTERED_SOURCE`; registered never received = `UNKNOWN`. Stale receipt does not date an outage; queue/disk are last reported, not current hardware state. Store failure or inconsistent registration/diagnostic selection cannot yield a current claim. No join from diagnostics to accepted measurements.

Battery read resolves effective Site basis and configured asset/binding IDs. Return `UNKNOWN` physical condition with named missing accepted causal temperature/capacity/stress inputs, or `NOT_CONFIGURED`/Unknown reason if absent/unbound. SOC and nameplate may appear only under separate evidence/declared labels and exact accepted IDs. Withhold degradation, remaining life, stress score, warranty/replacement exposure and diagnosis. Enforce tenant/Site/asset 401/403/404; a foreign asset is never an empty healthy row.

# Architect answer - remaining W1 screen contracts (2026-10-16)

Authority: `.agent/architect-W1-remaining-request.md`; registry `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.json` (`sitework:1#a`, `health:2#a`, `asset:3#a/#b`, `evidence-analysis:2#a/#b`); v6.9 Site Work, Site Health, Asset Health and Evidence Analysis screens; Site-first execution plan; existing decisions named below. These are bounded architecture contracts, not implementation, copy approval or panel delivery. The mock's populated Actions, condition claims and cross-domain lists are illustrative.

## S6-03 - `A-action-index` / `sitework:1#a`

**Source and scope.** Add a read over persisted `action` and `action_event`, authenticated to one tenant and resolved Site. W1 is mini-grid Actions only. Select a half-open UTC creation window `[from,to)` of at most 31 days; `created_at >= from AND created_at < to` is the membership rule, irrespective of later events. Echo scope and display timezone. The stable row key is `(tenant_id, site_id, action_id)`; include Action code, creation time, exact origin Finding and FindingRevision IDs, latest selected event ID, event recorded time, reduced lifecycle, and the canonical and exact-event Action links. The detail/event selection uses **recorded** order, `(recorded_at,id)`, not `occurred_at`; a backdated observation does not move the row in the list. Default order is `created_at DESC, action_id ASC`, with bounded pages and an opaque cursor bound to tenant, Site, window, selection watermark and last sort key. No unreviewed priority, owner, due date, verification verdict or cross-domain/global Work aggregate is inferred from v6.9.

**Watermark and missingness.** Read one consistent DB snapshot; return `read_at`, max included Action creation/event recorded fence (or explicit zero-row fence), total, page cursor, selected window and `CURRENT` only if the scoped store read succeeds. A zero-row `CURRENT` means no **persisted Actions created in this Site/window**, not no Finding, recommendation, incident, maintenance or unrecorded work. Store unavailable, unauthorized scope or a page cursor whose bound fence changed never becomes an empty list: return a typed failure or invalid-cursor response and reload page one. For selected rows, show the state as of the fence and preserve exact origin/event links after reload. An event arriving after the first-page fence invalidates subsequent pages; do not splice states. Keep exact historical Action detail available separately.

**Refusals and reuse.** Apply `D-2026-10-09-pre-s3-claim-routes-and-actor` identity rules: 401 unauthenticated, 403 known forbidden scope, 404 foreign/nonexistent Site or root. Refuse invalid/unbounded windows and changed cursors; no generic empty success on DB failure. Reuse the Site-scoped Action columns and event ordering in `backend/assetops_backend/actions/adapters/postgres_action_store.py:43-48,184-200`; the Action detail, origin, basis, state and stable links in `backend/assetops_backend/actions/service.py:524-581`; and S6-01 lifecycle/actor authority (`D-2026-10-14-action-lifecycle-and-policy-application`, `D-2026-10-15-s601-action-store-and-ui`, `D-2026-10-15-s601-reopen`). The **new** producer is the Site/window index query, consistent selection/pagination and Site Work UI. The existing `actions_for_finding` is a Finding-scoped helper, not this index. The S4 FindingIndex decision `D-2026-10-15-a-finding-index` is the Site/window, cursor and zero-row precedent; it does not select Action rows.

**Fixtures and packaging.** Reuse `D-action-records` for a persisted positive row, adding two Actions at distinct creation times and a later/backdated event to prove order, selected state, exact FindingRevision/event links, same-basis pagination, Site isolation and reload. Add an independently asserted assessed zero-row Site/window and a changed-fence cursor case; a client-filtered empty page is insufficient. S6-03 can be **one S slice**: one small read and one list UI, then independent review and owner checkpoint. It closes only the Actions tab of `sitework:1`, with Incidents/Maintenance and global queues honest gaps.

## S7-01 - `A-S7-health` / `health:2#a` and `asset:3#a`

**Source and scope.** Site Health reads registered gateway/source identities and the most recent centrally stored `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` per gateway for the authenticated Site. Use the server receipt clock for contact freshness under declared cadence, and separately report the diagnostic's own `observed_at`, source contact times, queue depth/oldest age, clock offset/trust, disk pressure and their reported-fact age. Return gateway/source/diagnostic IDs, registration, received/observed clocks, cadence, `read_at`, selected diagnostic sequence and a per-gateway source watermark. A fresh receipt of an old diagnostic makes contact potentially `CURRENT`, while queue/device facts remain old or `UNKNOWN`; virtual Lab time never measures real network lag. Per-source contact is reported only when the diagnostic supplies that source's timestamp; gateway contact alone does not prove a device is connected. `CURRENT | STALE | UNKNOWN` applies to **contact**, not battery condition. `STALE_PROJECTION` is separate if any derived read lags the selected central diagnostic.

The battery asset detail is a separate Site/asset-scoped boundary read. Resolve the configured battery and binding from the effective S1 Site basis, and disclose the selected asset/configuration IDs. In W1, return `UNKNOWN` physical condition and explicit missing causal inputs (for example accepted temperature/capacity/stress history) unless a separately reviewed causal battery observation and rule actually exist. The asset may display accepted SOC or declared nameplate facts only under their own labels and exact accepted evidence/binding IDs; neither proves degradation or remaining life. Withhold replacement exposure, warranty-at-risk, stress score, diagnosis and date. An unbound or absent battery is `NOT_CONFIGURED`/`UNKNOWN` with reason, never a healthy asset or fabricated asset row. `asset:3#b` positive physical condition remains non-core and unmapped.

**Watermark, empty and refusal.** No registered gateway is `NO_REGISTERED_SOURCE`; registered but never received is `UNKNOWN` contact. A stale contact is not proof that an outage began at the last receipt; queue pressure is the last **reported** state, not current hardware pressure. Return a watermarked empty or Unknown with reason, not a green zero. Any unreadable central store, inconsistent registration/diagnostic selection or stale projection refuses a current operational claim. Apply tenant/Site/asset 401/403/404 rules. Do not join diagnostic facts to accepted measurement evidence or claim battery condition from the gateway's own health. Reuse central latest-diagnostic selection in `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/adapters/postgres_gateway_access.py:172-194`, cadence-based contact and diagnostic fields in `backend/assetops_backend/evidence/gateway_access.py:289-343`, and the existing Site gateway read route in `backend/assetops_backend/gateway_api.py:94-109`. The **new** pieces are the selected two-clock fact-age/trust interpretation, bounded Site Health UI and asset Unknown/missing-input read/UI. `D-2026-10-09-pre-s3-evidence-readiness-health` already governs the semantic split; this decision narrows S7's first panel.

**`D-edge-health-cases` and packaging.** Build persisted central diagnostic cases through registered gateway authentication: (1) received/current heartbeat, (2) no later receipt across the declared cadence threshold, (3) delayed old diagnostic received now with old reported facts, (4) growing queue and old queued item, (5) reported clock offset or untrusted clock, and (6) disk pressure/acquisition-stop then recovery. Preserve local sequence, diagnostic ID, server receipt and source reported time; reload the central read. Add never-received and wrong-Site cases plus battery configured-but-causal-input-missing; private Lab state cannot satisfy them. S7-01 needs a **non-UI/UI split** inside its one owner-facing slice: first review diagnostic fixture and two reads, then Health/Asset UI, desktop/narrow behavior, links and owner checkpoint. Do not wait for `I-sim-battery-cases` or a positive physical condition claim.

## S3-02 - `I-capability-read` / `evidence-analysis:2#b`

**Source and scope.** For one authenticated mini-grid Site, selected half-open UTC day and exact selected S3 dispatch window/interval `assessment_revision_id`, derive a versioned CapabilityReadiness/claim ceiling from that assessment's accepted public `EvidenceQuery`/`AnalyticInputSet`, role coverage, quality, cohort, effective Foundation/binding IDs, evidence IDs and S3 rule/limitations. `I-capability-read` returns per-required-role covered duration/quality, missing or conflicted spans, selected accepted IDs, rule and selection basis; `I-claim-limit-read` maps these to what the **selected** dispatch classification can support, including Candidate/Necessary/Indeterminate bounds and named reasons. Do not infer a physical capacity or treat configured capability as observed. The result is assessment-scoped; it cannot silently swap in latest readings or a different day/revision. Reuse `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/dispatch_read.py:123-166` for selected status, basis, coverage, watermarks, window/interval revisions and limitations, and `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/dispatch_read.py:178-202` for exact revision evidence/configuration lineage. The existing Evidence Analysis view in `frontend/src/sites/LedgerSiteEvidence.tsx:93-161,762-775` supplies S2's distinct no-conclusion/status explanation; the **new** read and UI join the S3 selected assessment and express its rule-backed limit.

**Currentness, empty and refusals.** Echo tenant/Site, day/window, `assessment_revision_id`, selected run/model/cohort, knowledge cutoff, selected Foundation/binding IDs, accepted-source and analytic watermarks, `built_at` and current `read_at`. A positive supported ceiling requires a current S3 selection on the exact requested basis, a complete rule-backed assessment of the relevant portion and accepted public role evidence. A current assessment can still have `LIMITED` or `UNKNOWN` portions; name missing-role, poor-quality, conflicting, open-bound, source/cohort or configuration reasons. No selected assessment is `UNASSESSED`, not a zero capability. Pending/failed/dirty or changed S3 selection is `PENDING`/`FAILED`/`STALE_PROJECTION`; show a prior exact revision as historical only and withhold a current positive statement. Reject a foreign Site/revision, a revision outside the selected window, unsupported rule version or absent accepted lineage; preserve the usual 401/403/404 identity boundary. S2's delivered `evidence-analysis:2#a` remains a separate explanation and does not close positive `#b`.

**Fixtures and packaging.** Reuse reviewed `I-sim-dispatch-cases` only after checking exact accepted IDs against this rule: one full Candidate-capability interval, one missing available-PV or PCS-discharge-capability interval, one poor-quality/conflicting interval and one late revision that withdraws currentness. If existing fixtures lack one case, add only that case via registered simulated gateway, ordinary Batch V2 ingestion and S3 assessment; do not use private simulator truth. Assert selected revision and Evidence drill-down after reload, wrong Site/window, stale selection, desktop/narrow and keyboard selection. S3-02 needs a **non-UI/UI split**: review the capability/claim read and fixture first, then the Evidence Analysis UI and owner checkpoint. No broad shared projections or cold-chain/EV role set is required.

Resolve only `A-action-index` and `A-S7-health` with the decision anchors below. `I-capability-read` is an implementation node and stays open until reviewed code; `D-edge-health-cases`, all S task nodes and all panel portions stay open until fixture, read, UI and review evidence exist.
